March 16, 2021                                           6:00 pm                              Electronic Meeting
Pursuant to Governor Lee’s Executive Orders No. 16 and subsequent orders, and the need to limit the community
spread of COVID-19, the March 16, 2021 Gallatin City Council Meeting will be held by electronic means. The meeting
will be live-streamed to protect the public health, safety and welfare of the Council and citizens of Gallatin. Join the
Zoom Meeting at https://us02web.zoom.us/j/86330202276, dial +1 470 381 2552 or +1 470 250 9358, and enter
Webinar ID: 863 3020 2276.

Tuesday, February 1.6, 202L

 Mayor Paige Brown called the meeting to order at 6:04 P.M.

Pursuant to Tennessee Governor Bill Lee's Executive Orders #1.6 and subsequent
orders, and the need to limit the community spread of COVID-l9, the February 16,202-1.
Gallatin City Council Meeting was held by electronic means. The meeting was live-
streamed to protect the public health, safety and welfare of the Council and citizens of
Gallatin. Instructions for public participation in the meeting were available on the
City's website.

Vice Mayor Fennell made motion to allow the meeting electronically; Councilwoman
Love seconded.

Mayor Brown requested a ro11 call vote to allow the meeting electronically and a roll call
of those present.

City Recorder Connie Kittrell called the roll call vote:

Vice Mayor Shawn Fennell                                                                 Yes/Present
Councilman John D. Alexander                                                             Did Not Vote (Technical Difficulties)
Councilman Steve Fann                                                                    Yes/Present
Councilwoman Eileen George                                                               Yes/Present
Councilman Craig Hayes                                                                   Yes/Present
Councilwoman Lynda Love                                                                  Yes/Present
Councilman Jimmy Overton                                                                 Did Not Vote (Technical Difficulties)

Motion passed with 5 ayes and 0 nays


City Engineer Nick Tuttle led the opening prayer

                                                                   RESOLUTION NO. R2I@2-15

A RESOLUTION OF THB CITY OF GALLATIN, SUMNER COUNTY, TENNESSBE,
ADOPTING A PLAN OF SBRVICES FOR THE ANNEXATION OF A 59.10 +/- ACRE
PARCEL (TAX MAp 114, PARCEL 016.03), LOCATED NORTH OF WILDCAT RUN AND
EAST OF DOUGLAS LANE, AND PROVIDING FOR AN EFFECTIVB DATB.

               WHERBAS, the City of Gallatin received a petition by a properly owner for the
annexation of a 59.10 *l- acre portion of a parcel; and

               WHEREAS' the area proposed for annexation to the City of Gallatin is substantially
contiguous to the corporate limits to the City and within the City's Urban Growth Boundary; and

              WHERBAS, Tennessee Code Annotated, $ 6-51-102(b) requires that a Plan of
Service be adopted by the governing body of a City prior to the passage of an annexation resolution;
and,

               WHBREAS, the Gallatin Municipal-Regional Planning Commission, pursuant to
Tenn. Code Ann. $ 6-51-102(b)(4), $ 6-51-107, $13-3-104, $ l3-3-301, and $ 13-4-103, reviewed
and did not recommend approval of this Plan of Service, as contained in GMRPC Resolution 2020-
144.

          NOW, THERBFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE CITY OF GALLATIN,
TENNESSEE, AS FOLLOWS:

       SECTION l. Pursuant to the provisions of Section 6-51-102(b), Tennessee Code
Annotated, there is hereby adopted, for the area described in Exhibit 'A', the Plan of Services
described in Exhibit 'B':

       SECTION 2. This Resolution sliall be effective upon adoption, tlre public welfare reqr"riring
such

IT IS SO ORDERED.

PRESENT AND VOTING

                                            EXHIBIT'A'
                 Legal Description - Tax Map Parcel 11411016.03, 59.10 +/- Acres

                                    TWIN EAGLES PHASE 15

  DESCRIPTION OF THE RANDELL JONES AND WIFE, BARBARA JONES PROPERTY:
                              59.l0 Acres+/-

The property described herein is located in the 9th Civil District of Sumner County, Tennessee;
situated on the east side of Douglas Lane, approximately 0.86 mile north of the intersection with
Tennessee Highway 25 (Red River Road); in detail as follows:

Beginning at a point in the centerline of Douglas Lane; thence with said centerline, N 08o I 7'41 " E a
distance of 350.02'; thence leaving the centerline of Douglas Lane and with the southern line of Billy
C. Duncan property , S 80o54'51" E a distance of 1213.34' to an iron rod; thence continuing with the
Duncan property the following three calls: S 01'11'51" E a distance of 101.63'to a point; thence S
80o54'51" E a distance of 359.73' to an iron rod; thence N 06o05'32" E a distance of 814.66'to an

                                         EXHIBIT'B'

PLAN OF SERVICE FOR TAX MAP 114, PARCEL 016.03 (ANNX-2020-0016) pursuant to
                    Tenn. Code Annotated $ 6-5f-102(b)

  A. Potable Water
     Water service will be provjded by Gallatin Public Utilities Department upon extension of
     water lines to the propefiy by the property developer. Currently, city water lines extend to
     the south and east of the properly boundaries. The property is now located within the
     service area of the White House Utilities District. Water service can be provided to
     accommodate the development by extending water lines through developer-funded and
     constructed water mains. The 171 homes proposed for the annexation property will
     generate an estimated flow of 772,989 gallons per month. The developer will be
     responsible for installing water lines throughout the site and for making any necessary
     improvements off site to ensure adequate water service levels.

  B. Sanitary Sewer
     The property is adjacent to a sanitary sewer line on Wildcat Run. Eight (8)-inch Gallatin
     Public Utility Department sanitary sewer main exists along Dottie Drive within Phase 14
     and will be extended by the developer north into Phase 15. The developer will be required
     to make improvements to the lines and associated infrastructure. Upon submittal of
     construction plans, any forcemain or gravity flow lines shall be indicated on the drawings.
     These improvements will be constructed in existing or proposed rights-of-way andlor
     utility easements. The proposed development will generate approximately 25,419 gallons
     per day of effluent conveyed by sanitary sewer distribution, trunk lines, lift station, and
     treatment capacity to the WWTP. The existing trunk line eventually flows via gravity to a
     lift station located along the East Fork of Camp Creek near Nashville Pike. Sanitary sewer
     service can be made available to accolnmodate the developrnent by extending sewer lines
     through developer-funded and -constructed sewer mains.

  C. Street Construction and Maintenance
     New Public roadways will be constructed by the developer within Twin Eagles Phase 15.
     Right-of-way dedicatiorr will be reqr"rired along Douglas Lane. Once accepted, tlie City will
     maintain streets withirr the developnrent.

  D. Solid Waste and Refuse Collection
     Upon annexation, the City of Gallatin will begin collecting solid waste in tlie annexation
     area based on the provisions set fortli in the Gallatin Municipal Code. Residents will also
     have access to city-operated Resource recovel'y programs and services.

  E. Planning and Zoning Services
     Upon annexation, the City of Gallatin will provide planning and zoning services, including
     zot'ringenforcement, to all of tlre 59.10 +/- acre annexed property.

  F. Building Inspections and Code Enforcement Services
                                                             03/16/21 City Council Meeting Agenda-Page 18

[[page 19]]
   Upon annexation, building and code inspection services will be provided by the City of
   Gallatin.

G. Fire Protection
   The Gallatin Fire Department will provide fire protection after the effective date of the
   annexation. Upon development of the properties, the developer will provide fire hydrants in
   the annexation area pursuant to NFPA Life Safety Standards as determined by the City's
   Senior Fire Inspector. Once accepted by the City, the fire hydrants will be maintained by
   the City of Gallatin. The GFD proposes the construction of Fire Station No. 7 in the
   vicinity of The Meadows development to serve growth occurring on the north side of town.
   This new station will require the proper number of assigned firefighters, apparatus, and
   equipment but will make it possible to maintain the current level of service for this
   development. The station will be developed in concert with the phasing of The Meadows
   development.

H. Police Protection
   After the effective date of the annexation, police patrol, response to calls, and other routine
   police services will be provided by the Gallatin Police Department. The Police Depanment
   indicated that additional police officers and departmental vehicles will be needed to provide
   service to this area.

I. Animal Control
   The Gallatin Police Department provides animal control services and enforces the City's
   animal control ordinances. Afterthe effective date of the annexation, these services will be
   available in the annexation area.

J. Recreational Facilities and Programs
   After the effective date of the annexation, the benefit and use of all recreational facilities and
   programs provided by the Leisure Services Department will be available to future residents
   of the property at the City's rates. Recreational facilities and programs will be provided with
   existing personnel, facilities, and resources. In addition, the development plarr includes open
   space tracts that may serve recreation needs. Common open space may be designated by the
   developer and the City to serve community recreation needs to meet future recreation level
   ofservice standards.

K. Street Lighting and Blectric Service
   The CLrrnberland Electric Mernbership Corporation (CEMC) currently services this area
   or"rtside of Gallatin city lirnits. The Gallatin Department of Electricity will provide service
   upon development of the property. Existing infrastructure will slrpport the proposed
   developmerrt. Electric service by GDE shall cornply with service design requirements
   including, where required, placing service lines underground. Upon completion of any future
   street construction and acceptance by the City, street lighting will be extended irrto tlie
   annexed area as required by GDE.

L. Gas
   Tlie Gallatin PLrblic Utilities Depaftment provides natural gas services to residential,
   business, arrd industrial customers in the City of Gallatin and outside the City's corporate
                                                              03/16/21 City Council Meeting Agenda-Page 19

[[page 20]]
   limits. An existing gas line is located in the vicinity of the property. The Gallatin Public
   Utilities Department may authorize the extension of natural gas service from this existing
   line into the property upon development at developer expense. Upon authorization to extend
   gas lines by Gallatin Public Utilities, the City will maintain and monitor such lines.

M. School System
   The City shall provide written notice to the Sumner County School System of the proposed
   annexation and proposed plan of development prior to the public hearing on the annexation
   resolution as required by Tenn. Code Annotated, Section 6-51-102(b)(2). The School Board
   has been notified of the pending annexation and proposed development plan.

A Resolution No. R2l 02-15 of the City of Gallatin, Sumner County, Tennessee, to adopt a Plan of

the annexation of 59.10 */- acres (Tax Map 114, Parcels 016.03) into the City of Gallatin; and
providing an effective date.



RECOMMENDATION:
On January 25,2021, the Gallatin Municipal-Regional Planning Commission did not recommend
approval of the annexation and plan of service (ANNX-2020-0016). The Planning Commission's
primary reasoning was because of the increased traffic it will generate on Douglas Lane, which is a
narrow country road. Twin Eagles needs another entrance for ingress/egress. Since there are no other
suitable roads to the north or east, the Planning Cornmission did not approve Twin Eagles Phase 15
annexation.

       Section 1. The City Council of the City of Gallatin does hereby rezone a16.78 *l- acre
portion of a parcel from the R20-Low Density Residential District to the PGC-Planned General
Commercial District, rezoning a2.05 *l- acre portion of a lot from the MU-Mixed Use District to the
PGC-Planned General Commercial District, rezoning a 3.80 */- acre portion of a parcel from the
R20-Low Density Residential District to the MRO-Multiple Residential and Office District, and
rezoning a 3.04 *l- acre portion of a lot from the MU-Mixed Use district to the MRO-Multiple
Residential and Office District with a Preliminary Master Development Plan for The Goat Farm
totaling 25.67 +l- acres as described in Exhibit'B'.

         Section 2. The City Council of the City of Gallatin does hereby approve, authorize and
direct the revision of the City's Official Zoning Atlas to show the classification for the area as hereby
rezoned.

       Section 3. All ordinances or parts of ordinances in conflict with the provisions of this
ordinance are hereby repealed to the extent of sr.rch conflict.

       Section 4. If any provision of this ordinance or the application thereof to any person or
circumstance is held invalid, the invalidity shall not affect other provisions or applications of this
ordinance which can be given without the invalid provision or application, and to this end the
provisions of this ordinance are declared severable.

       Section 5. This ordinance shall become effective irnmediately upon passage, the public
welfare requiring such.

DEPARTMENT: PLANNING DBPARTMENT                                                  AGENDA #

SUBJECT:
An Ordinance No. 021@2-5 of the City of Gallatin, Sumner County, Tennessee, rezoning two
parcels totaling 25.67 +/- acres to PGC-Planned General Commercial District and MRO-Multiple
Residential and Office District with a Preliminary Master Development Plan for The Goat Farm.

SUMMARY:
An Ordinance of the City of Gallatin, Sumner County, Tennessee, rezoning portions of two parcels,
consisting of 16.78 */- acres from R2O-Low Density Residential District to the PGC-Planning
General Commercial District; 2.05 +l- acres from the MU-Mixed Use District to the PGC-Planned
General Commercial District; 3.80 +/- acres from the R20-Low Density Residential District to the
MRO-Multiple Residential and Office District; and 3.04 */- acres from the MU-Mixed Use District
to the MRO-Multiple Residential and Office District with a Preliminary Master Development Plan
for The Goat Farm, located South of Nashville Pike and East of Shutes Lane; authorizing the
revision to the Official ZoningAtlas; repealing conflicting ordinances; providing for severability;
and providing an effective date.



RECOMMENDATION:
On January 25,2021, the Gallatirr Municipal-Regional Planning Commission recommended
approval of the rezoning and master development plan in GMRPC Resolution No. 2021-007
(zoNE-2020-0023).

                                       EXHIBIT'B'
 PLAN OF SERVICE FOR PROPOSED BOWLES PROPERTY DEVELOPMENT
             TAX MAP 113, PARCELS 001.00,002.00, & 045.00
      (ANNX-2020-0017) pursuant to Tenn. Code Annotated $ 6-51-102(b)

A. Potable Water
   The Public Utilities Department indicated that existing water infrastructure is available to
   support the proposed annexation and development. An extension of the water lines will be
   required to serve the proposed development. Detailed plans and specifications for water and
   sanitary sewer shall be submitted to the Gallatin Public Utilities Department for review and
   approval.

   The developer will be required to construct an off-site extension and on-site improvements
   of the water distribution system to ensure sufficient capacities for domestic use and fire
   protection for the proposed development.

B. Sanitary Sewer
   The Public Utilities Department indicated that existing sanitary sewer infrastructure is
   available to support the proposed annexation and development. An extension of the sanitary
   sewer lines will be required to serve the proposed development. Detailed plans and
   specifications for water and sanitary sewer shall be submitted to the Gallatin Public Utilities
   Department for review and approval.

   The developer will be required to extend the sanitary sewer collection system within and
   adjacent to the site from the existing system as the proposed development is constructed.

C. Street Construction and Maintenance
   New Public roadways will be constructed by the developer within the Bowles Propefty
   Developrnent. Once accepted, the City will maintain streets and other public requirements
   within the development. Private street will be maintained by the developer or assigned
   homeowner/property owner association.

D. Solid Waste and Refuse Collection
   Upon annexation, the City of Gallatin willbegin collecting solid waste in the annexation
   area based on the provisions set forth in the Gallatin Municipal Code. Residents and
   business will also have access to city operated resource recovery programs and services.

E. Planning and Zoning Services
   Planning and zoning services of the City of Gallatin are cunently provided in a limited
   capacity for the property because it is located in the Planning Region. Most services are
   provided by the county. Upon annexation, the city will provide all planning services to the
   property.

F. Building Inspections and Code Enforcement Services
   Building and code inspection services of the City of Gallatin are currently provided in a
   limited capacity in the annexation area because it is located in the Planning Region. Most




                                                            03/16/21 City Council Meeting Agenda-Page 50

[[page 51]]
   services are provided by the county. Upon annexation, the city will provide all building code
   and enforcement services to the property.

G. Fire Protection
   After the effective date of the annexation, the Gallatin Fire Department will provide fire
   protection. Upon development of the propefty, the developer will provide fire hydrants in
   the annexation area pursuant to NFPA Life Safety Standards as determined by the City's
   Senior Fire Inspector. Once accepted by the City, the fire hydrants will be maintained by
   the City of Gallatin. Upon annexation, the property will be primarily served by Gallatin
   Fire Station #3, which has the appropriate number of personnel and number of department
   vehicles to service this development. Fire Station #3 is locate d'/o mile from the main
   residential structure in the center ofthe property.

   The Fire Department indicated that no additional firefighters, facilities, or vehicles would
   be required to provide service to the proposed development.

H. Police Protection
   After the effective date of the annexation, police patrol, response to calls, and other routine
   police services will be provided by the Gallatin Police Department. The Police Department
   indicated that an additional police officer and departmental vehicle will be needed to provide
   service to this area. These resources will be allocated upon incremental development of the
   property as determined by the Police Department.

I. Animal Control
   The Gallatin Police Department provides animal control services and enforces the City's
   animal control ordinances. After the effective date of the annexation, these services will be
   available in the annexation area.

J. Recreational Facilities and Programs
   After the effective date of the annexation, the benefit and use of all recreational facilities and
   programs provided by the Leisure Services Department will be available to existing and
   future residents of the property at the City's rates. Recreational facilities and programs will
   be provided with existing personnel, facilities, and resources. In addition, the development
   plan includes open space tracts that may serve recreation needs. Common open space may
   be designated by the developer and the City to serve community recreation needs to meet
   future recreation level ofservice standards.

K. Street Lighting and Electric Service
   Gallatin Department of Electricity indicated that adjacent electrical facilities will need to be
   upgraded and new facilities will need to be extended into the annexed area. The developers
   must meet all GDE underground and commercial policies. This includes providing all
   associated fees for line extensions. Any of GDE's existing facilities that are required to be
   relocated will be at the developer's expense. The developer will be responsible for
   coordinating relocations and associated expenses of existing utilities that belong to entities
   other tlian GDE. Upon completion of any future street construction and acceptance by the
   City, street lighting will be provided into the annexed area as required by GDE.




                                                              03/16/21 City Council Meeting Agenda-Page 51

[[page 52]]
L. Gas
   The Public Utilities Department indicated that existing gas infrastructure is available to
   support the proposed annexation and development. An extension of the gas lines by the
   developers will be required to serve the proposed development.

M. School System
   The City has provided written notice to the Sumner County School System of the proposed
   annexation and proposed plan of development prior to the public hearing on the annexation
   resolution as required by Tenn. Code Annotated, Section 6-51-102(bX2).

                                                                      RESOLUTION NO. R2Io2-I4

A RESOLUTION OF THE CITY OF GALLATIN, SUMNER COUNTY, TENNESSEE,
ANNEXING A THREE (3) PARCELS, TOTALING 655.44 +/- ACRES (TAX MAP 113
PARCELS 001.00, 002.00, & 045.00), LOCATED NORTH OF RED RMR ROAD AND
EAST OF HIGHWAY 109, INTO THE CITY OF GALLATIN; AUTHORIZING THE
ANNEXED PROPERTY TO BE INDICATED ON THE OFFICIAL ZONING ATLAS;
ASSIGNING THE ANNEXED AREA TO A COUNCIL DISTRICT; REPEALING
CONFLICTING ORDINANCES; PROVIDING FOR SEVERABILITY, AND PROVIDING
FOR AN EFFECTIVE DATE.

        WHEREAS, the owner of the affected area described in Exhibit 'A' submitted a petition to
annex three (3) parcels, totaling 655.44 */- acres, substantially contiguous to the City of Gallatin city
limits and located within the City of Gallatin Planning Region; and

       WHEREAS, a public hearing was held following public notice as prescribed by Tenn. Code
Ann. $ 6-51-102 and

         WHEREAS, the City of Gallatin deems it reasonable and mutually beneficial to annex the
territory described herein; and

        WHEREAS, the Gallatin Municipal-Regional Planning Commission has by a unanimous
vote recommended approval of GMRPC Resolution No. 2020-169 for the proposed annexation and
Plan of Service to the affected area; and

       WHEREAS' the City Council of the City of Gallatin has approved a Plan of Service for the
annexation area as described in Resolution No. R2142-13.

       NOWO THEREFORE' BE IT RESOLVED BY THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY
OF GALLATIN, SUMNER COUNTY, TBNNESSEE as follows:

       Section   l.
                  The City Council of the City of Gallatin does hereby annex the following property
as described in Exhibit'A'and indicated on the map in Exhibit'B';

        Section 2. The City Council of the City of Gallatin does hereby approve, authorize and direct
the revision of the City's boundaries and the Official Zoning Atlas to show the classification for the
area as hereby being annexed into the City as shown on Exhibit 'A' and Exhibit 'B' attached hereto.

       Section 3. The teritory described in Exhibit 'A' and depicted in Exhibit 'B' shall become
part of City Council District 5.

       Section   4. All ordinarrces or resolutions or parts of ordinances or resolutions in conflict
with the provisions of this resolution are hereby repealed to the extent of such conflict.




                                                                 03/16/21 City Council Meeting Agenda-Page 54

[[page 55]]
       Section   5. If any provision of this resolution or the application thereof to any person or
circumstance is held invalid, the invalidity shall not affect other provisions or applications of the
resolution which can be given effect without the invalid provision or application, and to this end the
provisions of this ordinance are declared severable.

       Section 6. This resolution shall take effect after final passage, the public welfare requiring
such, per Tenn. Code Ann. $ 6-51-104.

IT IS SO ORDERED.

*/- acres (Tax Map 1 13, Parcels 001 .00, 002.00, and 045.00), located north of Red River Road and

SUMMARY:

Map 113, Parcels 001.00, 002.00, and 045.00) into the City of Gallatin; authorizingthe annexed
property to be indicated on the Official ZontngAtlas; assigning the annexed area to a Council
District; repealing conflicting ordinances; providing for severability; and providing an effective date


RECOMMENDATION:

approval of the annexation in GMRPC Resolution No. 2020-169. (ANNX-2020-0017)

AN ORDINANCE OF THE CITY OF GALLATIN, SUMNER COUNTY, TENNESSEE,
REZONING THREE PARCELS, TOTALING 655.44 +/- ACRES, FROM THE RR-RURAL
RESIDENTIAL (COUNTY) DISTRICT TO THE R-8 (PRD) - MEDIUM DENSITY
RESTDENTTAL (PLANNED RESTDENTIAL DEVELOPMENT) DISTRICT (435.6 +/-
ACRES), FROM THE RURAL RESIDENTIAL (COUNTY) DISTRICT TO THE MU-MIXED
usE DISTRICT (132.92 +/- ACRES), AND FROM THE RR-RURAL RESIDENTIAL
(couNTy) DTSTRICT TO THE PBP-PLANNED BUSINESS PARK DISTRTCT (86.92 +/-
ACRES) WITH A PRELIMINARY MASTER DEVELOPMENT PLAN FOR THE BOWLES
PROPERTY, LOCATED NORTH OF RED RIVER ROAD AND EAST OF HIGHWAY 109;
AUTHORIZING THE REVISION TO BE INDICATED ON THE OFFICIAL ZONING
ATLAS; REPEALING CONFLICTING ORDINANCES; PROVIDING FOR
SEVERABILITY, AND PROVIDING FOR AN EFFECTIVE DATE.
        WHEREAS, the owner of the property submitted a complete application to rezone 655.44
*/- acres with a Preliminary Master Development Plan for the Bowles Property, located in the City
of Gallatin, Tennessee; and

       WHEREAS, the zoning map amendment conforms to the general plan of the area including
the established North Gallatin Emerging Business Park Character Area and Emerging Walkable
Community Character Area and that the public necessity, convenience, and general welfare, will be
served by approving the proposed amendment; and

        WHEREAS, the properly proposed for rezoning has adequate public utilities, infi'astructure
and private or municipal services necessary to serve the existing and proposed development and
population permitted by the requested zoning or such necessary services and facilities will be
provided upon developrnent of the property; and

        WHEREAS, the rezoning will be compatible with the surrounding environmerrt and will
protect tlie pr-rblic health, safety and welfare and will not be injurious to other property or
improvements irr the area in which the property is located; and

      WHERBAS, the Gallatin Municipal-Regional Planning Commission reviewed arrd
recomnrended approval of the proposed rezoning with Prelirninary Master Development Plan in
GMRPC Resolution No. 2020-170; and

      WHEREAS, a public hearing was held following pLrblic notice as prescribed by the Gallatin
ZoningOrdinance and Tenn. Code Ann. $ 13-7-203; and

        WHEREAS, the City Cor-rncil approved by majority vote ofthe members present the rezoning
request ofthe described property; and




                                                               03/16/21 City Council Meeting Agenda-Page 66

[[page 67]]
      NOWO THEREF'ORE' BE IT ORDAINED BY THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY
OF GALLATIN, SUMNER COUNTY' TENNBSSBE as follows:

        Section 1. The City Council of the City of Gallatin does hereby rezone a portion of three
parcels, totaling 655.44 */- acres, from the RR-Rural Residential (County) to the R-8 (PRD) -
Medium Density Residential (Planned Residential Development) District (435.6 +/- acres), from the
RR-Rural Residential (County) District to the MU-Mixed Use Districl (132.92 */- acres), and from
the RR-Rural Residential (County) District to the PBP-Planned Business Park District (86.92 +l-
acres) with a Preliminary Master Development Plan for the Bowles Property as described in Exhibit
rB'.

       Section   2. The City Council of the City of Gallatin does hereby approve, authorize and
direct the revision of the City's Official ZoningAtlas to show the classification for the area as hereby
rezoned.

       Section   3. All ordinances or parts of ordinances in conflict with the provisions of this
ordinance are hereby repealed to the extent ofsuch conflict.

       Section   4. If any provision of this ordinance or the application thereof to any person or
circumstance is held invalid, the invalidity shall not affect other provisions or applications of this
ordinance which can be given without the invalid provision or application, and to this end the
provisions ofthis ordinance are declared severable.

       Section 5. This ordinance shall becorne effective immediately upon passage, the public
welfare requiring such.

                 Parcel D. This can only be shown as a right-in/right-out on State Route 25 (Red
                 River Road).
              2. Revise all roadway cross sections to have a minimum 30" curb and gutter section
                 (24" gutter pan) so a portion ofthe curb inlets are not located in the asphalt
                 pavement.
              3. Add the following note to the plan "Review and approval for construction plans,
                 stormwater calculations, and traffic impact studies will be handled under
                 separate cover. Alterations made by these approved sets must be reflected on
                 this PMDP/FMDP. These revisions may include, but are not limited to changes
                 in lot layout, roadway configuration, and revision to open spaces."

                 permissible in the parcel from the MU zoning uses.

                 Preliminary Master Development Plan to the Planning Department.

                                         EXHIBIT'B'

PLAN OF SERVICB FOR TAX MAP 135, PARCEL 032.01 (ANNX-2020-0015) pursuant to
                    Tenn. Code Annotated $ 6-5f-102(b)

  A. Potable Water
     Water service will be provided by Gallatin Public Utilities Department upon extension of
     water lines to the property by the property developer. Currently, city water lines extend to
     the adjacent property to the east. This property (Parcel 32.00) is located within the Gallatin
     Public Utilities service area. Water service can be provided to accommodate the
     development by extending water lines through developer-funded and constructed water
     mains. The 122 homes proposed for the annexation propefty will generate an estimated
      flow of 767,974 gallons per month. The developer will be responsible for installing water
     lines throughout the site and for making any necessary improvements off site to ensure
     adequate water service levels.

  B. Sanitary Sewer
     The property is adjacent to a property (Parcel 32.00) served by an existing eight (8)-inch
     sanitary sewer line along Woods Ferry Road. Force mains will be constructed by the
     developer to pump effluent to this existing line. The developer will be required to make
     improvements to the lines and associated infrastructure. Upon submittal of construction
     plans, all forcemain and gravity flow lines shall be indicated on the drawings. These
     improvements will be constructed in existing or proposed rights-of-way andlor utility
     easements. The proposed development willgenerate approximately 511,983 gallons per
     day of effluent conveyed by sanitary sewer distribution, trunk lines, lift station, and
     treatment capacity to the WWTP. The existing trunk line eventually flows via gravity to a
     lift station located fafther down Woods Ferry Road near State Route 109. Sanitary sewer
     service can be made available to accommodate the development by extending sewer lines
     through developer-firrrded and -constructed sewer mains.

  C. Street Construction and Maintenance
     New PLrblic roadways will be constructed by the developer witliin Woods Crossing. Once
     accepted, the City will maintairr streets within the development.

  D. Solid Waste and Refuse Collection
     Upon annexation, the City of Gallatin will begin collecting solid waste in the annexation
     area based on the provisions set forth in the Gallatirr MunicipalCode. Future residents will
     also lrave access to city-oper'ated Resource recovery programs and services.

  E. Planning and Zoning Services
     Upon annexation, the City of Gallatin will provide planning and zoning services, inclLrding
     zoning enforcentent, to all of the 31 .378 *l- acre annexed property.




                                                              03/16/21 City Council Meeting Agenda-Page 85

[[page 86]]
F. Building Inspections and Code Enforcement Services
   Upon annexation, building and code inspection services will be provided by the City of
   Gallatin.

G. Fire Protection
   The Gallatin Fire Department will provide fire protection after the effective date of the
   annexation. Upon development of the properties, the developer will provide fire hydrants in
   the annexation area pursuant to NFPA Life Safety Standards as determined by the City's
   Senior Fire Inspector. Once accepted by the City, the fire hydrants will be maintained by
   the City of Gallatin. The Fire Department indicated that no additionalresources and
   manpower are needed to adequately service the area. Gallatin Fire Station No. 5, the
   nearest station, is approximately a half road mile away from the Woods Ferry entry point
   and response times to this entrance will be within the national best practice standard of four
   (4) minutes.

H. Police Protection
   After the effective date of the annexation, police patrol, response to calls, and other routine
   police services will be provided by the Gallatin Police Depafiment. The Police Department
   indicated that no additional police officers and deparlmental vehicles will be needed to
   provide service to this area.

I. Animal Control
   The Gallatin Police Depafiment provides anirnal control services arrd enforces the City's
   anirnal control ordinances. Afterthe effective date of the annexation, these services will be
   available in the annexation area.

J. Recreational Facilities and Programs
   After the effective date of the annexation, the benefit and use of all recreational facilities and
   programs provided by the Leisure Services Departnrent will be available to future residents
   of the property at the City's rates. Recreational facilities and progratns will be provided with
   existing personnel, facilities, and resources. In addition, the developnterrt plarr inclr-rdes open
   space tracts that rnay serve recreation needs. Cornnron open space may be designated by the
   developer and tlie City to serve comrnunity recreation needs to meet future recreatiorr level
   ofservice standards.

K. Street Lighting and Electric Service
   The Gallatin Deparlment of Electricity will provide service uporr development of the
   property. Existing infiastrLrcture will slrppofi tlre proposed developrnent after upgrades.
   Electric service by GDE shall comply with service design requirernents irrclLrding, where
   required, placing service lines underground. Upon completion of any future street




                                                              03/16/21 City Council Meeting Agenda-Page 86

[[page 87]]
   construction and acceptance by the City, street lighting will be extended into the annexed
   area as required by GDE.

L. Gas
   The Gallatin Public Utilities Depaftment provides natural gas services to residential,
   business, and industrial customers in the City of Gallatin and outside the City's corporate
   limits. An existing gas line is located in the vicinity of the property. The Gallatin Public
   Utilities Department may authorize the extension of natural gas service from this existing
   line into the property upon development at developer expense. Upon authorization to extend
   gas lines by Gallatin Public Utilities, the City will maintain and monitor such lines.

M. School System

   The City shall provide written notice to the Sumner County School System of the proposed
   annexation and proposed plan of development prior to the public hearing on the annexation
   resolution as required by Tenn. Code Annotated, Section 6-51-102(bX2). The School Board
   has been notified of the pending annexation and proposed development plan.

r"tuin the ability to plan for areas outside of the city boundaries.

AN ORDINANCE OF THE CITY OF GALLATINO SUMNER COUNTY, TENNESSEE,
AMENDING GALLATIN MUNICIPAL CODE, CHAPTER 2, ARTICLE III, DIVISION 3,
PLANNING COMMISSION, SECTION 2.I5I, CREATION AND MEMBERSHIP AND
SECTION 2-IS3,ADDITTONAL POWERS

       WHEREAS, on April 2, 1974, the Tennessee State Planning Commission, in accordance
with Tenn. Code Ann. g 13-202 (now $ 13-3-102), granted a request made by the City of Gallatin
and designated the Gallatin Municipal Planning Commission as the Gallatin Municipal - Regional
Planning Commission for its future planning and for the purposes of controlling the zoning and
subdivision of certain territory outside said City; and

        WHEREAS, the Tennessee Department of Economic and Community Development now
serves the role of the State Planning Commission, per Tenn. Code Ann., $ 13-3-101(a); and

       WHEREAS, in accordance with Tenn. Code Ann. $ 13-3-102, the acceptance of a
designation of a municipal planning commission as a regional planning commission is not
obligatory, but is subject to the consent of the designated municipal planning commission; and

       WHEREAS, Sumner County created and established a county planning commission, a
county zoning resolution, county subdivision regulations o and a professional engineering and
planning staff and, therefore, demonstrated the means of conserving or controlling the physical
ievelopment of the territory located outside of the corporate limits of the City of Gallatin; and

       WHEREAS, the Gallatin Regional-Municipal Planning Commission recommended
deactivating its role and authority as a regional planning commission and dissolution of the Gallatin
planning Region with the intention of the City to establish a Municipal Planning Commission per
Tennessee CodeAnnotated, $ 13-4-101 et. seq.; and

       WHEREAS, deactivating the regional planning authority is deemed desirable for the
operation of planning and zoning administration of the City of Gallatin and the immediate
surrounding area, and

        WHEREAS, the Gallatin Zoning Ordinance, the Gallatin Subdivision Regulations and
                                                                                     Commission
other regulations adopted and approved by the Gallatin Municipal - Regional Planning
shall reirain applicable and the iolicy for development within the municipal boundaries
                                                                                        until such
time that these are amended io reflect the       jurisdiction of the Gallatin Municipal  Planning
Commission, and

        WHEREAS, in accordance with Tenn. Code Ann. $ 13-3-102 and $ l3-4-10I, the City
Council of the City of Gallatin requests the Commissioner of the Tennessee Department
                                                                                               of
                                                                                        Planning
Economic and Community Development, with the approval of the        Local Government
                                                                       planning authority of the
Advisory Committee, to approve a resolution to deactivate the regional
                               planning Commission and to dissolve the Gallatin Planning Region,
Gallatin Municipal - it.gio"ut
and

       WHEREAS, the Tennessee Department of Economic and Community Development Local
Government Planning Advisory Committee intends to consider a resolution to deactivate the
regional planning authority of the Gallatin Municipal - Regional Planning Commission and to
dissolve the Gallatin Planning Region.

    NOW THEREFORE, BE IT ORDAINED BY THE CITY OF GALLATIN,
TENNESSEE, pursuant to Tenn. Code Ann. $ 13-4-101 et. seq. and based on approval of a
resolution by the Local Government Planning Advisory Committee of the Tennessee Department
of Economic and Community Development to deactivate the Gallatin Municipal - Regional
Planning Commission and to dissolve the Gallatin Planning Region, the following sections of the
Gallatin Municipal Code are hereby amended as follows:

Section L.     Chapter 2, Administration, Article III, Boards, Committees, Commissions,
Division 3, Planning Commission.

   (a) Sec 2-151. Creation and Membership is amended as follows:
       Pursuant to the provision of T.C.A. $13-4-101 et. seq. there is hereby created a municipal
       planning commission, hereinafter referred to as the planning commission. The planning
       commission shall consist of seven (7) members. Two (2) of these members shall be the
       mayor and a councilmember selected by the city council. The other five (5) members shall
       be appointed by the mayor shall be for five (5) years each. The terms of the mayor and the
       councilmember selected by the council shall run concurrently with their terms of office. Any
       vacancy in an appointive membership shall be filled for the unexpired term by the mayor or
       council as described herein.

   (b) Sec 2-I53. - Additional Powers is amended by the following:
       The municipal planning commission shall have the powers granted by state law relating to
       municipal planning commissions.

Section 2.     Application of the Zoning Ordinance and other advisory roles

        Upon adoption of this ordinance the duties and responsibilities assigned to the Gallatin
        Municipal- Regional Planning Commission described in the Gallatin Zoning Ordinance,
        Gallatin Subdivision Regulations and other advisory roles is hereby assigned to the Gallatin
        Municipal Planning Commission.

        BE IT FURTHER ORDAINED BY THE CITY OF GALLATIN, TENNESSEE, thAt
this ordinance shall take effect immediately upon adoption of a resolution by the Tennessee
Department of Economic and Community Development Local Government Planning Advisory
Committee to deactivate the regional planning authority of the Gallatin Municipal - Regional
planning Commission and to disiolve the Gallatin Planning Region, the public welfare requiring
such.

a 2.04 *l- acre portion of the adjacent abandoned railroad right-of-way fiom the RR - Rural
Residential District (County) to the R-8 PRD - Medium Density Residential Planned Residential
Development District and rezone 1.06 +/- acre portion of abandoned railroad right-of-way from the
RR-Rural Residential District (County) to the IR-lndustrial Restrictive District, as described in

in Exhibit'B'.

       Section   2. The City Council of the City of Gallatin does hereby approve, authorize and
direct the revision of the City's Official ZoningAtlas to show the classification for the area as hereby
rezoned.

       Section   3. All ordinances or parts of ordinances in conflict with the provisions of this
ordinance are hereby repealed to the extent ofsuch conflict.

        Section 4. If any provision of this ordinance or the application thereof to any person or
circumstance is hcld invalid, the invalidity shall not affect other provisions or applications of this
ordinance which can be given without the invalid provision or application, and to this end the
provisions of this ordinance are declared severable.

       Section 5. This ordinance shall become effective immediately upon passage, the public
welfare requiring such.

Clearing and Grubbing: Payment will be made based upon percentage overall area of
work cornplete and paid as a lump sum item.


Mass Grading of Site (LS): Payment will be made based upon percentage overall area of
work complete and paid as a lurnp sum item. ln the event rock is encountered in
excavation, an additional price for rock will apply as follows:

Solid Rock (CI/: Non-degradable rock that can not be economically excavated by the
proper use of a power shovel or without tlre use of explosives. Paynent will be made
based upon the cubic yard (CY) measure of rock excavated. Measurement methods will
be agreed upon by contractor and City prior to any rock excavation.

Fragmented Rock (CY): Non-degradable rock that can be excavated by the proper use of
a power shovel, hydraulic hammer, and ripping methods, and does not require the use of
explosives. Payment will be made based upon the cubic yard (CY) measure of rock
excavated. Measurement methods will be agreed upon by contractor and City prior to
any rock excavation.

Bio Retention Areas: Payment will be made based upon percentage of work complete and
paid as a lump sum itcm per specific Bio Retention Area.

not specified herein regarding this Contract. Contractor, by signature of its authorized
representative, hereby acknowledges that he/she has read this Contract, understands
